=== KPI #3: Decision Speed (DQN vs Rule-Based) ===
Generated: 25-Sep-2026 14:07:35

Decision latency of both policies, the link quality each achieves on the same
closed-loop runs, and (section 5) recovery time in decision cycles from the
episodic closed loop with dwell/hysteresis.

DQN latency/recovery/agreement are averaged across the full SNR sweep
(each threat has one data point per SNR point in the diagnostic run).

Threat                     DQN (ms)    Rule (ms)   Agree% DQN rec (clean) Rule rec (clean)
jamming                       0.990      0.00012     100%          99.2%          99.2%
reactive_jamming              1.127      0.00008     100%          99.4%          99.4%
sweeping_jammer               1.080      0.00009      67%          87.4%          82.4%
noise_burst                   1.124      0.00013      50%          48.0%          56.1%
path_loss                     1.085      0.00011     100%          69.0%          69.0%
spoofing                      1.022      0.00010      37%          99.7%          99.7%
antenna_fault                 1.127      0.00013      90%          99.0%          89.0%
benign_interference           1.056      0.00015      33%            N/A            N/A
none                          0.970      0.00016     100%            N/A            N/A

Mean DQN latency:  1.065 ms
Mean Rule latency: 0.00012 ms
Speed ratio: Rule-Based is ~8956x faster than DQN (lookup table vs neural net inference)
DQN-vs-Rule agreement (overall, all threats x SNR): 75.2%

NOTE: both policies act through the same physical countermeasure model
(apply_countermeasure.m, D28), so agreement and recovery compare decision
quality only. Disagreement is no longer cosmetic: actions differ in effect.

Interpretation: the rule is effectively instant (a lookup); the DQN costs about a
millisecond of inference, small next to the ~10 ms detection path. Both are far
faster than the dwell of a few frames, so recovery time is set by detection and
hysteresis rather than by the policy's compute time (section 5).

--- Recovery time in decision cycles (run_closed_loop_episodes.m, D31) ---
Threat onset mid-stream; dwell 3 cycles, hold 10 cycles; medians in cycles after onset.
T_recover over episodes the threat actually degraded and no countermeasure was active at onset.
configuration              T_act T_recover   recovered   pre-cfg switches  goodput
dqn | hysteresis               3         8     66/91           0     1.57     0.89
dqn | no hysteresis            1        15     21/48          40    18.60     0.87
rule | hysteresis              3         8     68/90           4     1.43     0.85
rule | no hysteresis           1         6     14/25          61     6.98     0.91

--- KPI #3: DQN vs Rule-Based (decision latency, recovery time, link quality) ---
Source: results/kpi3_measurement.txt
=== KPI #3: Decision Speed (DQN vs Rule-Based) ===
Generated: 25-Sep-2026 14:07:35

Decision latency of both policies, the link quality each achieves on the same
closed-loop runs, and (section 5) recovery time in decision cycles from the
episodic closed loop with dwell/hysteresis.

DQN latency/recovery/agreement are averaged across the full SNR sweep
(each threat has one data point per SNR point in the diagnostic run).

Threat                     DQN (ms)    Rule (ms)   Agree% DQN rec (clean) Rule rec (clean)
jamming                       0.990      0.00012     100%          99.2%          99.2%
reactive_jamming              1.127      0.00008     100%          99.4%          99.4%
sweeping_jammer               1.080      0.00009      67%          87.4%          82.4%
noise_burst                   1.124      0.00013      50%          48.0%          56.1%
path_loss                     1.085      0.00011     100%          69.0%          69.0%
spoofing                      1.022      0.00010      37%          99.7%          99.7%
antenna_fault                 1.127      0.00013      90%          99.0%          89.0%
benign_interference           1.056      0.00015      33%            N/A            N/A
none                          0.970      0.00016     100%            N/A            N/A

Mean DQN latency:  1.065 ms
Mean Rule latency: 0.00012 ms
Speed ratio: Rule-Based is ~8956x faster than DQN (lookup table vs neural net inference)
DQN-vs-Rule agreement (overall, all threats x SNR): 75.2%

NOTE: both policies act through the same physical countermeasure model
(apply_countermeasure.m, D28), so agreement and recovery compare decision
quality only. Disagreement is no longer cosmetic: actions differ in effect.

Interpretation: the rule is effectively instant (a lookup); the DQN costs about a
millisecond of inference, small next to the ~10 ms detection path. Both are far
faster than the dwell of a few frames, so recovery time is set by detection and
hysteresis rather than by the policy's compute time (section 5).

--- KPI #4: False Alarm Rate (FAR), non-hostile classes ---
Source: results/far_measurement.txt
Definition (D29): an action on a non-hostile link whose BER <= 2x clean. Denominator: healthy-link trials. Bound (D35): FAR <= 5% with the 95% upper limit below it, for Eb/N0 >= the KPI #1 threshold (0 dB).
  Eb/N0    0 dB: FAR 0/60 = 0.0% [0.0, 6.0]
  Eb/N0    4 dB: FAR 0/30 = 0.0% [0.0, 11.4]
  Eb/N0   10 dB: FAR 0/30 = 0.0% [0.0, 11.4]
  Above threshold (pooled): FAR 0/120 = 0.0%, 95% upper limit 3.1% (Wilson; Rule of Three 2.5%) -> MET
Full per-class breakdown: see results/far_measurement.txt
